Gauge-invariant magnetic charges in linearised gravity
Chris Hull, Maxwell L. Hutt, Ulf Lindström

Abstract
Linearised gravity has magnetic charges carried by (linearised) Kaluza-Klein monopoles. A gauge-invariant expression is found for these charges that is similar to Penrose’s gauge-invariant expression for the ADM charges. A systematic search is made for other gauge-invariant charges.



Preprint:
Imperial-TP-2024-CH-04

UUITP-11/24

1
Introduction

For the linearised theory of the graviton in Minkowski space, each Killing vector of Minkowski space gives an invariance of the theory and there is a corresponding conserved charge
Q[k]
that can be constructed via a linearisation of the ADM construction
[
1
]
.
In Ref.
[
2
]
analogous ‘electric’ conserved charges
Q[\kappa]
,
Q[\lambda]
were constructed in spacetime dimensions
d\geq 5
for the dual graviton theory
[
3
,
4
]
corresponding to certain generalised Killing tensors
\kappa
,
\lambda
that give the invariances of the dual theory. These were then reinterpreted as ‘magnetic’ charges for the graviton theory, for which they are topological charges.

In Ref.
[
5
]
, covariant 2-form currents

Y[K]_{\mu\nu}=R_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}K^{\alpha\beta}

(1)

were introduced and shown to be conserved in regions without sources provided that
K_{\alpha\beta}
is a conformal Killing-Yano (CKY) tensor.
Integrating these over a closed codimension-2 surface then gives conserved charges, which we refer to as Penrose charges.
These were studied in Ref.
[
6
]
and shown to give a linear combination of
Q[k]
,
Q[\lambda]
and a topological charge, with
k
and
\lambda
constructed from
K
.
For the graviton theory, the Penrose charges can be interpreted as giving a covariantisation of
Q[k]
. However, the Penrose charges can also be interpreted in the dual graviton theory as a covariantisation of the charge
Q[\lambda]
. We will discuss the charges in the dual graviton theory in a forthcoming publication and will here focus on the charges in the graviton theory.

Kaluza-Klein monopoles play an important role in string theory
[
7
]
and carry a gravitational magnetic charge
[
8
]
.
The magnetic charge
Q[\kappa]
in the linearised theory is the charge carried by linearised Kaluza-Klein monopoles
[
2
]
.
The magnetic charge
Q[\kappa]
, however, is not related to the Penrose charges.
One of the aims of this paper is to find a covariant current that gives this charge.
This leads us to generalise the Penrose construction and seek conserved 2-form currents, linear in the Riemann tensor, of the form

\Omega[V]_{\mu\nu}=R^{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta}V_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta|\gamma\delta}

(2)

for some rank-6 tensor
V_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta|\gamma\delta}
with the symmetries
1
1

1

We use a vertical bar to separate groups of fully antisymmetric indices.

V_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta|\gamma\delta}=V_{[\mu\nu\alpha\beta]|[\gamma\delta]}

(3)

We investigate the conditions on
V
for this current to be conserved, classifying the full set of such conserved 2-forms.
One of our main results is that, as well as the Penrose charges, the
\kappa
-type charges can also be constructed as a subset of these charges, and so also have a covariant expression.

In the quantum theory, gauge-invariant operators are particularly important. Finding covariant forms for electric and magnetic gravitational charges, which is our goal here, then gives gauge invariant topological operators that generate continuous generalised symmetries.

One motivation of this work is to further understand the generalised symmetries of the free graviton theory, following work by
[
9
]
. There, it was shown that any system containing specific electric and magnetic ‘bi-form symmetries’ which participate in a particular mixed ’t Hooft anomaly will have a gapless phase with a spin-2 mode in the IR spectrum. In other words, the graviton can be viewed as the Nambu-Goldstone boson associated with the non-linear realisation of such symmetries. Similar ideas were used in
[
10
]
to show that certain anomalies cannot be realised by any local, unitary theory. Finding gauge-invariant expressions for the conserved quantities related to all the (generalised) symmetries of the theory and understanding their anomalies is, therefore, a promising avenue to gain insight into the IR physics at play.
Other work on generalised symmetries in gravity can be found in
[
11
,
12
,
13
,
14
,
15
]
.

Consider first the symmetries corresponding to the Killing vectors
k
. For each
k
there is a 1-form current

j[k]_{\mu}\equiv T_{\mu\nu}k^{\nu}

(4)

where the energy-momentum tensor
T_{\mu\nu}
is the source for the linearised Einstein equation.
This can be integrated over a co-dimension one surface to give a conserved charge that generates a 0-form symmetry. However, there is a ‘secondary current’
J[k]_{\mu\nu}
which satisfies

\partial^{\nu}J[k]_{\mu\nu}=j[k]_{\mu}

(5)

so that this 2-form current is conserved wherever the source
j
vanishes. However, the current
J[k]
is not uniquely determined by (
5
). The ADM construction reviewed in
[
2
]
gives a non-covariant
J[k]
, but the Penrose construction gives a covariant current.

Integrating
J
over a codimension 2 surface gives a conserved charge which in the quantum theory gives a topological operator – it is unchanged under deformations of the surface that do not pass through regions in which
j\neq 0
. This charge generates a 1-form symmetry.
Note that whereas for non-linear gravity the surface needs to be taken at spatial or null infinity as gravitational energy-momentum is non-local, for the linearised theory it can be any surface as the energy-momentum is local.

There is a similar story for the
\kappa
and
\lambda
charges
[
2
]
.
In the dual theory, there is a primary 1-form current
j
for each, and a corresponding 2-form current
J
with
\partial^{\nu}J_{\mu\nu}=j_{\mu}
, with corresponding charges and 0-form and 1-form symmetries, similarly to the case above.
The current
J
gives rise to a 2-form current in the graviton theory, giving a topological charge there.
In this paper we will focus on the charges and postpone a discussion of the corresponding generalised symmetries to a future paper.

Given the relevance of these charges to Kaluza-Klein monopoles, we also study the gauge-invariant charges constructed from
\Omega[V]
on toroidally compactified spaces
\mathbb{R}^{1,D-1}\times T^{n}
with
D+n=d
, giving them a unified higher-dimensional origin.

Conventions

We refer to tensors whose indices can be split into fully anti-symmetric groups of lengths
n_{1},n_{2},\dots,n_{r}
as
{\lfloor n_{1},n_{2},\dots,n_{r}\rfloor}
-tensors. For example, a tensor with index symmetries
M_{\mu\nu\rho|\sigma|\alpha}=M_{[\mu\nu\rho]|\sigma|\alpha}
is a
{\lfloor 3,1,1\rfloor}
-tensor. Such tensors have been studied in Refs.
[
16
,
17
,
18
,
19
,
20
,
21
]
where they are sometimes referred to as ‘multi-forms’ and in the case of
r=2
they are often called ‘bi-forms’.
Unless otherwise stated, these tensors are in
reducible

GL(d,\mathbb{R})
representations. That is, they transform in a representation which is labelled by a tensor product of single-column Young tableaux of lengths
n_{1},n_{2},\dots,n_{r}
.

Irreducible

GL(d,\mathbb{R})
representations are instead labelled by
{[n_{1},\dots,n_{r}]}
(note the different type of bracket).
The criterion for a reducible
{\lfloor p,q\rfloor}
-tensor,
N
, to transform in an irreducible
GL(d,\mathbb{R})
representation (i.e. one labelled by a single Young tableau with two columns of lengths
p
and
q
) is

N_{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{p}|\nu_{1}\dots\nu_{q}}=N_{[\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{p}]|[\nu_{1}\dots\nu_{q}]},\quad N_{[\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{p}|\nu_{1}]\nu_{2}\dots\nu_{q}}=0

(6)

where we have assumed
p>q
. If
p=q
then furthermore
N
satisfies

N_{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{p}|\nu_{1}\dots\nu_{p}}=N_{\nu_{1}\dots\nu_{p}|\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{p}}

(7)

Irreducible representations of
GL(d,\mathbb{R})
can be viewed as reducible representations of
SO(1,d-1)\subset GL(d,\mathbb{R})
and can, therefore, be decomposed into a sum of irreducible representations of
SO(1,d-1)
. A
{[p,q]}
-tensor
A
which transforms in an irreducible representation of
SO(1,d-1)
labelled by a Young tableau with two columns of lengths
p
and
q
must satisfy eqs. (
6
) and (
7
), and must also be traceless with respect to the
SO(1,d-1)
-invariant Minkowski metric
\eta_{\mu\nu}
:

\eta^{\mu_{1}\nu_{1}}A_{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{p}|\nu_{1}\dots\nu_{q}}=0

(8)

The Young symmetriser
\mathcal{Y}
is a linear operator which projects a tensor onto a given irreducible representation. We will denote in the superscript whether the projection is to an irreducible
GL(d,\mathbb{R})
or
SO(1,d-1)
representation. We will denote in the subscript the Young tableau of the irreducible representation which is being projected on to. For example,
\mathcal{Y}_{{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to2.71053pt{\vss\hbox to2.3917pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-2.71053pt\vrule height=2.71053pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 2.3917pt\vrule height=2.71053pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-2.71053pt\hrule width=3.02936pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 2.3917pt\hrule width=3.02936pt,height=0.31883pt}&\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to2.71053pt{\vss\hbox to2.3917pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-2.71053pt\vrule height=2.71053pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 2.3917pt\vrule height=2.71053pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-2.71053pt\hrule width=3.02936pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 2.3917pt\hrule width=3.02936pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to2.71053pt{\vss\hbox to2.3917pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-2.71053pt\vrule height=2.71053pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 2.3917pt\vrule height=2.71053pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-2.71053pt\hrule width=3.02936pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 2.3917pt\hrule width=3.02936pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 690.4pt}}$}}}^{SO}
is the projector onto the irreducible
{[2,1]}
representation of
SO(1,d-1)
(so the resulting tensor is traceless).

We will use some of the
operations on
{\lfloor p,q\rfloor}
-tensors introduced in
[
16
]
.
2
2

2

A similar formalism was introduced in
[
17
]
.

The left and right exterior derivatives of a reducible
{\lfloor p,q\rfloor}
-tensor
N
are

\displaystyle(\differential_{L}A)_{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{p+1}|\nu_{1}\dots\nu_{q}}

\displaystyle=\partial_{[\mu_{1}}A_{\mu_{2}\dots\mu_{p+1}]|\nu_{1}\dots\nu_{q}}

(9)

\displaystyle(\differential_{R}A)_{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{p}|\nu_{1}\dots\nu_{q+1}}

\displaystyle=A_{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{p}|[\nu_{1}\dots\nu_{q},\nu_{q+1}]}

(10)

where a comma denotes a partial derivative. These are nilpotent,
\differential_{L}^{2}=\differential_{R}^{2}=0
, and commute,
[d_{L},d_{R}]=0
.
N
is
\differential_{L}
-closed if
\differential_{L}N=0
and
\differential_{L}
-exact if
N=\differential_{L}M
for some
M
.
The
\differential_{L}
-cohomology of
{\lfloor p,q\rfloor}
-tensors on a flat space is the set of
\differential_{L}
-closed
{\lfloor p,q\rfloor}
-tensors modulo those which are
\differential_{L}
-exact.
We define
\differential_{R}
-closed and
\differential_{R}
-exact tensors, and so
\differential_{R}
-cohomology, analogously.

We also define left and right duality operations
\star_{L}
and
\star_{R}
by

\displaystyle(\star_{L}A)_{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{d-p}|\nu_{1}\dots\nu_{q}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{p!}\epsilon_{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{d-p}\alpha_{1}\dots\alpha_{p}}A^{{{\alpha_{1}\dots\alpha_{p}}\mathchoice{\makebox[23.27776pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[23.27776pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[14.31514pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[11.49307pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[21.56284pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[21.56284pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[14.455pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[11.61385pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{|\nu_{1}\dots\nu_{q}}}}

(11)

\displaystyle(\star_{R}A)_{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{p}|\nu_{1}\dots\nu_{d-q}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{q!}\epsilon_{\nu_{1}\dots\nu_{d-q}\alpha_{1}\dots\alpha_{q}}A^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[23.26495pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[23.26495pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[15.45201pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[12.32599pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\alpha_{1}\dots\alpha_{q}}}}_{{{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{p}|}\mathchoice{\makebox[21.46074pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[21.46074pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[14.38206pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[11.54091pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}

(12)

which satisfy
\star_{L}^{2}=(-1)^{p(d-p)+1}
and
\star_{R}^{2}=(-1)^{q(d-q)+1}
. Finally, there are
adjoint derivatives
\differential_{L}^{\dagger}
and
\differential_{R}^{\dagger}
which we define by

\displaystyle(\differential_{L}^{\dagger}A)_{\mu_{2}\dots\mu_{p}|\nu_{1}\dots\nu_{q}}

\displaystyle=\partial^{\mu_{1}}A_{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{p}|\nu_{1}\dots\nu_{q}}

(13)

\displaystyle(\differential_{R}^{\dagger}A)_{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{p}|\nu_{2}\dots\nu_{q}}

\displaystyle=\partial^{\nu_{1}}A_{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{p}|\nu_{1}\dots\nu_{q}}

(14)

which are also nilpotent
(\differential_{L}^{\dagger})^{2}=(\differential_{R}^{\dagger})^{2}=0
.
N
is
\differential_{L}
-coclosed if
\differential_{L}^{\dagger}N=0
and
\differential_{L}
-coexact if
N=\differential_{L}^{\dagger}M^{\prime}
for some
{\lfloor p+1,q\rfloor}
-tensor
M^{\prime}
. There are analogous definitions for
\differential_{R}^{\dagger}
.

2
The charges of the graviton

We consider the Fierz-Pauli theory of a massless spin-2 field

h_{\mu\nu}
propagating on a background spacetime
\mathcal{M}
. This is taken to be a
d
-dimensional Minkowski space, possibly with some points or regions removed so as to allow for non-trivial topology. In later sections we also consider toroidal compactifications of some spatial directions. Here we present only a summary of various conserved charges which can be associated with the graviton, following
[
2
,
6
]
. The reader should refer to Appendix
A
for detailed definitions and to Ref.
[
6
]
for details.

2.1
Penrose charges, ADM charges and magnetic charges

We define a linearised connection
\Gamma_{\mu\nu|\rho}
which is a [2,1]-tensor with components

\Gamma_{\mu\nu|\rho}=\partial_{[\mu}h_{\nu]\rho}

(15)

The graviton has gauge transformations

h_{\mu\nu}\to h_{\mu\nu}+\partial_{(\mu}\xi_{\nu)}

(16)

under which the linearised Riemann tensor

R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma}=\partial_{\rho}\Gamma_{\mu\nu|\sigma}-\partial_{\sigma}\Gamma_{\mu\nu|\rho}

(17)

is gauge-invariant. In the linearised theory,
\Gamma_{\alpha\beta|\gamma}
is related to the linearised Christoffel connection by a gauge transformation.

The ADM charges in linearised gravity have a surface integral representation
[
1
]

Q[k]=\int\star J[k]

(18)

where
k
is a Minkowski space Killing vector, and
J[k]
is the ‘secondary current’ which satisfies

\partial^{\nu}J[k]_{\mu\nu}=G_{\mu\nu}k^{\nu}\equiv j[k]_{\mu}

(19)

so is conserved when
R_{\mu\nu}=0
. If the field equation
G_{\mu\nu}=T_{\mu\nu}
holds, then
J[k]
is conserved away from sources. The explicit form of
J[k]
is given in Appendix
A
. In regions with sources, the result is non-vanishing and gives the 1-form ‘primary’ conserved current
j[k]
which has been referred to as the Einstein current in Ref.
[
22
]
.

As mentioned in the previous section, the ADM charges can be related to the covariant Penrose charges which are integrals of the Penrose 2-form (
1
). This 2-form is constructed from a CKY tensor, which satisfies

\partial_{\lambda}K_{\mu\nu}=\tilde{K}_{\lambda\mu\nu}+2\eta_{\lambda[\mu}\hat{K}_{\nu]}

(20)

with

\tilde{K}_{\lambda\mu\nu}=\partial_{[\lambda}K_{\mu\nu]}\quad\mbox{and}\quad\hat{K}_{\mu}=\frac{1}{d-1}\partial^{\nu}K_{\nu\mu}~.

(21)

For comparison with analysis which will be used later, we
give a brief representation theory derivation of the result
that the 2-form
K
in the Penrose 2-form
Y[K]
must be a CKY tensor. Demanding conservation of
Y[K]
implies that

0=\partial^{\mu}Y[K]_{\mu\nu}=\partial^{\mu}(R_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}K^{\alpha\beta})=R_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\partial^{\mu}K^{\alpha\beta}

(22)

where we have used the contracted Bianchi identity
\partial^{\mu}R_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}=0
. This follows from the Bianchi identity
\partial_{[\mu}R_{\alpha\beta]\gamma\delta}=0
and the vacuum field equation
R_{\mu\nu}=0
. Now we must impose a condition on
\partial^{\mu}K^{\alpha\beta}
such that
R_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\partial^{\mu}K^{\alpha\beta}
vanishes. This can be done via a decomposition into irreducible representations of
SO(1,d-1)
as follows.

Firstly,
\partial_{\mu}K_{\alpha\beta}
is a
GL(d,\mathbb{R})
tensor in the reducible
{\lfloor 1,2\rfloor}
representation. This is a tensor product representation, labelled by Young tableaux
{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to3.39996pt{\vss\hbox to3.00003pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-3.39996pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-3.39996pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt}}$}}\otimes{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to3.39996pt{\vss\hbox to3.00003pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-3.39996pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-3.39996pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to3.39996pt{\vss\hbox to3.00003pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-3.39996pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-3.39996pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt}}$}}
, which can instead be written as a direct sum of irreducible
GL(d,\mathbb{R})
representations. These can be seen as
reducible
representations of
SO(1,d-1)\subset GL(d,\mathbb{R})
. Decomposing further into
irreducible
representations of
SO(1,d-1)
by separating out traces, we have

\left({\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to3.39996pt{\vss\hbox to3.00003pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-3.39996pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-3.39996pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt}}$}}\otimes{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to3.39996pt{\vss\hbox to3.00003pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-3.39996pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-3.39996pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to3.39996pt{\vss\hbox to3.00003pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-3.39996pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-3.39996pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt}}$}}\,\right)_{GL}=\left(\,{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-3.39996pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to3.39996pt{\vss\hbox to3.00003pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-3.39996pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-3.39996pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to3.39996pt{\vss\hbox to3.00003pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-3.39996pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-3.39996pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt}}$}}\oplus{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-3.39996pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-3.39996pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\kern 690.4pt}}$}}\,\right)_{GL}=\left(\,{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to3.39996pt{\vss\hbox to3.00003pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-3.39996pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-3.39996pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to3.39996pt{\vss\hbox to3.00003pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-3.39996pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-3.39996pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt}}$}}\oplus{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to3.39996pt{\vss\hbox to3.00003pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-3.39996pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-3.39996pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to3.39996pt{\vss\hbox to3.00003pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-3.39996pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-3.39996pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to3.39996pt{\vss\hbox to3.00003pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-3.39996pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-3.39996pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\kern 690.4pt}}$}}\oplus{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to3.39996pt{\vss\hbox to3.00003pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-3.39996pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-3.39996pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt}}$}}\right)_{SO}

(23)

where the
SO(1,d-1)
representations are traceless by definition (as well as satisfying the index symmetries in eqs. (
6
) and (
7
)). This can be written out explicitly for the tensor
\partial_{\mu}K_{\alpha\beta}
as

\partial_{\mu}K_{\alpha\beta}=\tilde{K}_{\mu\alpha\beta}+\left(\partial_{\mu}K_{\alpha\beta}-\tilde{K}_{\mu\alpha\beta}-2\eta_{\mu[\alpha}\hat{K}_{\beta]}\right)+2\eta_{\mu[\alpha}\hat{K}_{\beta]}

(24)

Here, the first term on the right-hand side is in the

representation, the term in parentheses is in the traceless

representation and the final term involves a vector in the

representation. Inserting this decomposition into eq. (
22
), the first and last terms vanish immediately from the Bianchi identity
R_{[\mu\nu\alpha]\beta}=0
and the field equation
R_{\mu\nu}=0
. Therefore, the result vanishes if and only if we impose that the term in parentheses in eq. (
24
) vanishes by itself. Indeed, this immediately gives the CKY equation (
20
). We see from this that the CKY equation can be equivalently written

\mathcal{Y}_{{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to2.71053pt{\vss\hbox to2.3917pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-2.71053pt\vrule height=2.71053pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 2.3917pt\vrule height=2.71053pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-2.71053pt\hrule width=3.02936pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 2.3917pt\hrule width=3.02936pt,height=0.31883pt}&\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to2.71053pt{\vss\hbox to2.3917pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-2.71053pt\vrule height=2.71053pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 2.3917pt\vrule height=2.71053pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-2.71053pt\hrule width=3.02936pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 2.3917pt\hrule width=3.02936pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr\lower 0.31883pt\vbox{\kern 0.00002pt\kern 0.15941pt\hbox{\kern 0.31883pt\vbox to2.71053pt{\vss\hbox to2.3917pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-2.71053pt\vrule height=2.71053pt,width=0.31883pt\kern 2.3917pt\vrule height=2.71053pt,width=0.31883pt}\kern-0.15941pt\kern-2.71053pt\hrule width=3.02936pt,height=0.31883pt\kern 2.3917pt\hrule width=3.02936pt,height=0.31883pt}\cr}}\kern 690.4pt}}$}}}^{SO}(\partial K)=0

(25)

Arguments similar to these will be essential to our more general treatment in section
7
.
This approach to defining generalised Killing tensors by representation theory was developed and applied to a variety of tensors in Ref.
[
21
]
.

An important result for analysing the Penrose charges is the following Minkowski space decomposition of a CKY tensor

[
23
,
21
,
9
]
:

K_{\alpha\beta}=\mathcal{A}_{\alpha\beta}+\mathcal{B}_{[\alpha}x_{\beta]}+\mathcal{C}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}x^{\gamma}+2x_{[\alpha}\mathcal{D}_{\beta]\gamma}x^{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}\mathcal{D}_{\alpha\beta}x_{\gamma}x^{\gamma}

(26)

where
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
,
\mathcal{C}
, and
\mathcal{D}
are constant antisymmetric tensors.
This is the most general form of a CKY tensor in Minkowski space.
When the Penrose charges are constructed from
K
as in eq. (
1
), the
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{D}
terms are related to ADM charges of the graviton, while the
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{C}
terms are related to magnetic charges of the graviton. It follows from this decomposition that
\hat{K}
is a Killing vector and
\tilde{K}
is a closed CKY 3-form on Minkowski space; that is, it satisfies

\partial_{\mu}\tilde{K}_{\alpha\beta\gamma}=\frac{3}{d-2}\eta_{\mu[\alpha}\partial^{\lambda}\tilde{K}_{\beta\gamma]\lambda}

(27)

We therefore define a Killing vector

k_{\mu}=2(d-3)\hat{K}_{\mu}

(28)

and a closed CKY 3-form

\tilde{\lambda}_{\mu\nu\rho}=(-1)^{d-1}(d-1)\tilde{K}_{\mu\nu\rho}

(29)

with coefficients chosen for later convenience. The
\mathcal{B}
- and
\mathcal{D}
-type CKY tensors in eq. (
26
) give non-zero
k
, while the
\mathcal{C}
- and
\mathcal{D}
-type CKY tensors give non-zero
\tilde{\lambda}
. Notably, the
\mathcal{A}
-type CKY tensors have
k=0
and
\tilde{\lambda}=0
.

There are also dual ADM charges which stem from certain invariances of the dual graviton field
[
2
]
. The dual graviton,
D_{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{d-3}|\nu}
, is a mixed symmetry gauge field of rank
{[d-3,1]}
which describes the same degrees of freedom as the graviton
h_{\mu\nu}
.
The Killing vectors of Minkowski space, which have a crucial role in the ADM charges (
18
) of the graviton, are gauge parameters which leave the graviton field invariant on all field configurations and so correspond to global symmetries. Similarly, there are certain gauge transformations which leave the dual graviton invariant on all field configurations. These are analogous to the isometries generated by Killing vectors in the graviton theory, and are similarly understood as global symmetries.
The tensorial parameters for these can be viewed as generalised Killing tensors.

The associated charges can (sometimes) be written in terms of the graviton field and interpreted as magnetic charges for the graviton
[
2
]
. This is most easily done with a particular choice of gauge for the graviton, so that the resulting magnetic charges found in
[
2
]
are given in that particular gauge.

In
d=4
, the dual graviton is a symmetric tensor gauge field and its charges are somewhat distinct to the case
d>4
. Here we focus on
d>4
and will then discuss
d=4
in section
2.2
.
The magnetic charges are of two types, with generalised Killing tensors denoted
\lambda
and
\kappa
.

In the dual graviton theory,
\lambda
is required to be a Killing-Yano (KY)
(d-3)
-form. That is, it satisfies

\partial_{\mu}\lambda_{\nu_{1}\dots\nu_{d-3}}=\partial_{[\mu}\lambda_{\nu_{1}\dots\nu_{d-3}]}

(30)

It is a property of rank-
p
KY forms that their Hodge dual is a closed CKY
(d-p)
-form
[
24
,
21
]
. We will denote the Hodge dual of
\lambda
by
\tilde{\lambda}
, which is then a closed CKY 3-form (i.e. satisfies eq. (
27
)).

The other type of generalised Killing tensors are the
\kappa
tensors. In the dual theory, these are
{[d-4,1]}
-tensors and so satisfy

\kappa_{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{d-4}|\nu}=\kappa_{[\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{d-4}]|\nu}

(31)

and

\kappa_{[\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{d-4}|\nu]}=0

(32)

Furthermore, they satisfy a differential constraint

\partial_{[\sigma}\kappa_{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{d-4}]|\nu}=0

(33)

Charges can be constructed from these generalised Killing tensors as integrals of 2-form secondary currents
J[\lambda]
and
J[\kappa]
, similarly to eq. (
18
). We will refer to these as
\lambda
-charges and
\kappa
-charges respectively.
The secondary current
J[\lambda]
satisfies

\partial^{\nu}J[\lambda]_{\mu\nu}=\frac{(-1)^{d-1}}{d-1}\tilde{\lambda}^{\alpha\beta\gamma}R_{\mu\alpha\beta\gamma}\equiv j[\lambda]_{\mu}

(34)

and so is conserved by the Bianchi identity
R_{\nu[\alpha\beta\gamma]}=0
. An explicit expression for
J[\lambda]
is given in Appendix
A
, while
J[\kappa]
will be discussed in section
3
. In the dual description, magnetic sources can be introduced which have the effect of sourcing the Bianchi identity, so the ‘primary’ current
j[\lambda]
can be non-zero
[
2
]
. In the present work we consider only the original graviton formulation of the theory and therefore treat
R_{\nu[\alpha\beta\gamma]}=0
as an identity.

The key result of Ref.
[
6
]
is that the Penrose 2-form is related to the secondary currents
J[k]
and
J[\lambda]
by

Y[K]_{\mu\nu}=J[k]_{\mu\nu}+J[\lambda]_{\mu\nu}+\partial^{\rho}Z[K]_{\mu\nu\rho}

(35)

where
Z[K]
is a 3-form given by

Z[K]^{\mu\nu\rho}=12K^{[\mu\nu}\Gamma^{{{\rho\beta]}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.7304pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.7304pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.13268pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.9519pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[10.9473pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[10.9473pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.66605pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.76144pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{|\beta}}}

(36)

Eq. (
35
) has been used to give a covariant generalisation of the ADM charges, and the charges associated with the
\lambda
-invariances in Ref.
[
6
]
.

In
d>4
, the Penrose charges associated with the
\mathcal{A}
- and
\mathcal{C}
-type CKY tensors (i.e. the KY tensors) vanish on Minkowski space
[
6
,
12
]
, leaving only the
\mathcal{B}
- and
\mathcal{D}
-type charges which are related to the ADM charges. The
\kappa
-charges, however, are not related to the Penrose 2-form.
One of the aims of this work is to find a manifestly gauge-invariant current which agrees with
J[\kappa]
in the gauge used in
[
2
]
. This would give a gauge-invariant generalisation of
J[\kappa]
that can then be used in any gauge.

2.2
Four dimensions

Four dimensions are special due to the fact that the graviton and dual graviton are both symmetric tensor gauge fields, as well as certain properties of CKY tensors.
Since the dual graviton is also a symmetric rank-2 gauge field in
d=4
its invariances are also given by the Killing vectors of Minkowski space. Therefore, in this case, there are no independent
\lambda
and
\kappa
tensors and these are only relevant for the dual graviton in
d>4
. More precisely, the
\lambda
tensors reduce to Killing vectors when
d=4
and the
\kappa
tensors simply become constant 1-forms (which are also Killing vectors). So the
\lambda
- and
\kappa
-charges discussed above degenerate in four dimensions.

Some of the charges associated with invariances of the four-dimensional dual graviton can be written in terms of the graviton and expressed as a surface integral of a secondary current
\tilde{J}[\tilde{k}]
which is given in Appendix
A
. Here
\tilde{k}
is another Killing vector, independent of
k
, which can be written in terms of a rank-2 CKY tensor as
\tilde{k}=\star\differential{K}
(whereas
k\propto\star\differential\star K
).
Then, in
d=4
, the Penrose 2-form is related to the secondary currents by

Y[K]_{\mu\nu}=J[k]_{\mu\nu}+\tilde{J}[\tilde{k}]_{\mu\nu}+\partial^{\rho}Z[K]_{\mu\nu\rho}

(37)

Therefore, again in
d=4
, the Penrose charges give a gauge-invariant generalisation of the ADM and dual ADM charges.

2.3
Penrose 2-form as a special case of
\Omega[V]

We will later consider the more general family of conserved 2-forms
\Omega[V]
introduced in eq. (
2
).
At this point, we simply demonstrate that this more general set of 2-forms includes the Penrose 2-form
Y[K]
for a specific choice of
V
. Namely, choosing
V
to be given by
3
3

3

This form of
V[K]
is closely related to the form of the Kastor-Traschen current for a KY tensor
K
(see Appendix of Ref.
[
25
]
).

V[K]^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[18.60477pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[18.60477pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[11.2791pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.05649pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{|\gamma\delta}}}_{{{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[12.03123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.03123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.07574pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.33981pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}=6\delta^{\gamma\delta}_{[\mu\nu}K_{\alpha\beta]}

(38)

where
\delta^{\gamma\delta}_{\mu\nu}\equiv\delta^{[\gamma}_{[\mu}\delta^{\delta]}_{\nu]}
and
K
is a CKY 2-form yields

\Omega[V]_{\mu\nu}=6R^{{{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[7.83525pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[7.83525pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.71463pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.3676pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\gamma\delta}}}\delta^{\gamma}_{[\mu}\delta^{\delta}_{\nu}K_{\alpha\beta]}=6R^{{{\alpha\beta}\mathchoice{\makebox[11.1355pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.1355pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.73412pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.81006pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[9.73317pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[5.9061pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.21864pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{[\mu\nu}}}K_{\alpha\beta]}=R_{\mu\nu\alpha\beta}K^{\alpha\beta}=Y[K]_{\mu\nu}

(39)

where we have used that
R_{\mu\nu}=0
in the penultimate equality. Hence, this particular choice of
V
reduces
\Omega[V]
to the Penrose 2-form.

3
\kappa
-invariances and their associated charges

As mentioned in the previous section, for
d\geq 5
the dual formulation of linearised gravity has two types of invariances corresponding to two different types of tensors,
\lambda
and
\kappa
. We now discuss the
\kappa
tensors in more detail. These are
{[d-4,1]}
-tensors
\kappa
satisfying eqs. (
31
), (
32
) and (
33
).
In particular, eq. (
32
) ensures the irreducibility of
\kappa
under
GL(d,\mathbb{R})
. In the dual theory, this is essential for the dual graviton field itself to remain irreducible. The irreducibility of the dual graviton is necessary for the two descriptions of linearised gravity to describe the same number of degrees of freedom. This irreducibility implies that
\kappa
transforms under a
GL(d,\mathbb{R})
representation labelled by a Young tableau with two columns of lengths
d-4
and 1.

We define the dual of a
\kappa
tensor, which we will denote
\tilde{\kappa}
, by

\tilde{\kappa}=\star_{L}\kappa

(40)

which has components

\tilde{\kappa}_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta|\nu}=\frac{1}{(d-4)!}\epsilon_{\alpha\beta\gamma\delta\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{d-4}}\kappa^{{{\mu_{1}\dots\mu_{d-4}}\mathchoice{\makebox[8.20526pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[8.20526pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.78163pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.70114pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[25.05835pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[25.05835pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[17.06831pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[14.33119pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{|\nu}}}

(41)

This is a
{\lfloor 4,1\rfloor}
-tensor,

\tilde{\kappa}_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma|\alpha}=\tilde{\kappa}_{[\mu\nu\rho\sigma]|\alpha}

(42)

which satisfies

\partial^{\mu}\tilde{\kappa}_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma|\alpha}=0

(43)

from eq. (
33
).
If
\kappa
is irreducible, i.e. if it satisfies eq. (
32
), then
\tilde{\kappa}
is traceless, i.e.
\tilde{\kappa}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[21.85051pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[21.85051pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[12.06741pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[8.61954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\sigma}}}_{{{\mu\nu\rho\sigma|}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.63394pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.79993pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.99994pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}=0
.

With a particular gauge choice for both the graviton theory and the dual graviton theory, the connection for the dual graviton theory is dual to the connection
\Gamma
for the graviton theory Ref.
[
2
]
.
For the graviton, this gauge choice is

\Gamma^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.06758pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.06758pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.73412pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.81006pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\nu}}}_{{{\mu\nu|}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}=0

(44)

In this gauge, the secondary current for the dual graviton theory associated with the
\kappa
-invariances can be written locally in terms of the graviton in this gauge
for all
\kappa
tensors as
[
2
]

J[\kappa]_{\mu\nu}=(-1)^{d}\frac{2}{d-1}\tilde{\kappa}_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma|\alpha}\Gamma^{\alpha\sigma|\rho}

(45)

which satisfies

\partial^{\nu}J[\kappa]_{\mu\nu}=\frac{(-1)^{d}}{d-1}\tilde{\kappa}_{\mu\alpha\beta\gamma|\sigma}R^{\sigma\alpha\beta\gamma}\equiv j[\kappa]_{\mu}

(46)

so is conserved by the Bianchi identity
R_{\sigma[\alpha\beta\gamma]}=0
.
As for
j[\lambda]
in eq. (
34
), the ‘primary’ current
j[\kappa]
can be non-zero when sources are included in the dual formulation of the theory. Here, we consider only the source-free case and so
j[\kappa]=0
identically.
Now, integrating over a codimension-2 cycle
\Sigma_{d-2}
gives a conserved charge
[
2
]

Q[\kappa]=\int_{\Sigma_{d-2}}\star J[\kappa]

(47)

which we refer to as a
\kappa
-charge.

The secondary current
J[\kappa]
in eq. (
45
) can be written locally as a total divergence

J[\kappa]_{\mu\nu}=\partial^{\rho}Z[\kappa]_{\mu\nu\rho}

(48)

with
Z[\kappa]
given by

Z[\kappa]_{\mu\nu\rho}=(-1)^{d}\frac{1}{d-1}\,\tilde{\kappa}_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma|\alpha}h^{\sigma\alpha}

(49)

This implies that the charge
Q[\kappa]
is topological and is non-zero only when
h_{\mu\nu}
is a non-globally defined field configuration.
Indeed, as
\star J[\kappa]
is closed, the charge
\int_{\Sigma_{d-2}}\star J[\kappa]
,
for a given cycle
\Sigma_{d-2}
,
depends only on the cohomology class of
\star J[\kappa]
and vanishes if it is an exact form. As locally

\star J=\differential(\star Z)
, it will be exact only if
\star Z
is a globally defined form, which will be the case if
h_{\mu\nu}
is globally defined. See Refs.
[
6
,
2
]
for further discussion.

Note that the secondary Noether current
J[\kappa]
derived in terms of the dual graviton in Ref.
[
2
]
can be written locally in terms of the graviton for any
\tilde{\kappa}
tensors satisfying eq. (
43
), whereas
J[\lambda]

can be written locally in terms of the graviton
if
\tilde{\lambda}
is constant.

We note from eq. (
49
) that
J[\kappa]
will be conserved whether
\kappa
is irreducible or not (i.e. whether
\tilde{\kappa}
is traceless or not). Therefore, as far as the charges constructed with
\kappa
are concerned, there is no need for
\kappa
to be irreducible and charges can equally well be defined if it is reducible.
The condition of irreducibility came from requiring the charge to result from an invariance of the dual graviton theory.
Since we work directly in the graviton theory in this paper we will
not
insist that
\kappa
satisfy eq. (
32
). That is, here we require only that
\kappa
is a
{\lfloor d-4,1\rfloor}
-tensor (rather than an irreducible
{[d-4,1]}
-tensor). A priori,
\tilde{\kappa}
is a
{\lfloor 4,1\rfloor}
-tensor. The reducible
{\lfloor 4,1\rfloor}
representation of
GL(d,\mathbb{R})
is a tensor product
{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to3.39996pt{\vss\hbox to3.00003pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-3.39996pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-3.39996pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 345.4pt}}$}}\otimes{\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to3.39996pt{\vss\hbox to3.00003pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-3.39996pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-3.39996pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\kern 345.4pt}}$}}={\hbox{$\vbox{\hbox{\vtop{\halign{&\opttoksa@YT={\font@YT}\getcolor@YT{\save@YT{\opttoksb@YT}}\nil@YT\getcolor@YT{\startbox@@YT\the\opttoksa@YT\the\opttoksb@YT}#\endbox@YT\cr\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to3.39996pt{\vss\hbox to3.00003pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-3.39996pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\vrule 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to3.39996pt{\vss\hbox to3.00003pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-3.39996pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-3.39996pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt}\cr}}\kern 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to3.39996pt{\vss\hbox to3.00003pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-3.39996pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt}\kern-0.19997pt\kern-3.39996pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\hrule width=3.7999pt,height=0.39993pt}&\lower 0.39993pt\vbox{\kern 0.19997pt\hbox{\kern 0.39993pt\vbox to3.39996pt{\vss\hbox to3.00003pt{\hss$$\hss}\vss}\kern-3.39996pt\vrule height=3.39996pt,width=0.39993pt\kern 3.00003pt\vrule
